**PORTFOLIO VOLATILITY**

**10.55% annualised**

**Basis:**
  - Method: sqrt(w'Σw) on a sample covariance matrix of daily returns
  - Window: 2025-09-19 to 2026-09-21, 252 closes
  - Weights: market value of invested assets, cash excluded, priced as of 2026-09-21
  - Annualisation: x sqrt(252)

**Not done.** Cash is excluded from the weights, so this is the
volatility of the invested assets rather than of the total
portfolio. It is one window under one regime; it is not an
average of the per-holding volatilities.

**The thesis**, W-1's, as my watchlist states it:

  Search and YouTube are the advertising businesses that keep growing through every cycle, the cloud segment is now profitable and taking share, and the company funds its own capital spending from operating cash flow with no debt to speak of. The thesis is that the advertising businesses keep their margins while the cloud segment's margin catches up, and that the capital spending on data centres shows in cloud revenue rather than in lasting margin damage.

Each claim below is the model's reading (claude-sonnet-5) of one section of Alphabet Inc.'s latest annual report, with the passage it rests on quoted from the filing; `stated` means the passage says it in so many words, `inferred` that it is read across the section.

**Item 1** of the 10-K for FY2025, accession 0001652044-26-000018, filed 2026-02-05, source EDGAR filing archive:
  [1.1] (stated) The company operates two main Google segments plus a separate group of non-Google businesses called Other Bets, with centralized AI research supporting all of them.
      "We report Google in two segments, Google Services and Google Cloud, and all non-Google businesses collectively as Other Bets."
  [1.2] (stated) Google Services earns most of its revenue from selling performance and brand advertising placed on Search, YouTube, and partner network sites.
      "Google Services generates revenues primarily by delivering both performance and brand advertising that appears on Google Search & other properties, YouTube, and Google Network partners' properties ("Google Network properties")."
  [1.3] (stated) Beyond advertising, Google Services also makes money from consumer subscription services, app store sales, and hardware device sales.
      "consumer subscriptions, which primarily include revenues from YouTube services, such as YouTube TV, YouTube Music and Premium, and NFL Sunday Ticket, as well as Google One, which offers access to our most capable Gemini models;"
  [1.4] (stated) Google Cloud generates revenue mainly by charging customers usage-based fees and subscriptions for infrastructure, platform, and application cloud services.
      "Google Cloud generates revenues primarily from consumption-based fees and subscriptions for infrastructure, platform, applications, and other cloud services."
  [1.5] (stated) Other Bets businesses, such as autonomous ride-hailing and internet services, earn revenue mainly by selling those transportation and internet offerings to customers.
      "Revenues from Other Bets are generated primarily from the sale of autonomous transportation and internet services."
  [1.6] (inferred) Customers keep using the company's products because of continual innovation and integration of advanced AI capabilities like Gemini models into widely used products.
      "Today, all 15 of our half-billion-user products — including seven with two billion users — use our Gemini models."
  [1.7] (inferred) Advertisers keep buying because the company's advertising tools use AI to help them find effective, measurable ways to reach customers.
      "Products like Demand Gen, Performance Max, and Product Studio use the full power of our AI to help advertisers find untapped and incremental conversion opportunities."
  [1.8] (stated) Cloud customers are attracted by a broad set of AI-optimized infrastructure, developer tools, security, data, and agent products that help them innovate and cut costs.
      "Google Cloud helps customers build for the future, improve productivity, reduce costs, and unlock new growth engines."
  [1.9] (stated) Users could stop using the company's products because competing products and services are easily accessible, making user loyalty fragile.
      "users, for whom other products and services are literally one click away, on the basis of the relevance of our advertising, as well as the general usefulness, security, and availability of our products and services;"
  [1.10] (stated) The business faces intense competition across nearly every product area, including search, advertising, cloud, AI, hardware, and social media, which could draw customers away.
      "We face formidable competition in every aspect of our business, including but not limited to, from:"
  [1.11] (stated) Growing and inconsistent government regulation around AI, privacy, competition, and content could raise costs, restrict offerings, or otherwise cause customers or the business to be harmed.
      "Our compliance with these laws and regulations may be onerous and could, individually or in the aggregate, increase our cost of doing business, make our products and services less useful, limit our ability to pursue certain business practices or offer certain products and services"
  [1.12] (stated) Advertisers could shift spending away if the company fails to keep delivering ads efficiently across changing channels and user preferences.
      "advertisers, primarily based on our ability to generate sales leads, and ultimately customers, and to deliver their advertisements in an efficient and effective manner across a variety of distribution channels even as trends in advertising mediums and user preferences change;"

**Item 1A** of the 10-K for FY2025, accession 0001652044-26-000018, filed 2026-02-05, source EDGAR filing archive:
  [1A.1] (stated) The company depends heavily on advertising revenue, so losing advertisers or partners to competitors, or failing to adapt to new ad formats shaped by AI, could damage its business and financial results.
      "Failing to provide superior value or deliver advertisements effectively and competitively could harm our business, reputation, financial condition, and operating results."
  [1A.2] (stated) Large-scale spending on AI infrastructure, data centers, and long-term leasing and commercial agreements creates risk that if partners fail to perform or an industry downturn occurs, the company could be left with unusable excess capacity and unpaid obligations.
      "In such nonperformance or an industry downturn, we may incur additional liabilities, have excess capacity that we cannot easily redeploy, and not receive payments from our counterparties or customers."
  [1A.3] (stated) Intense competition, especially in AI development, could allow rivals to out-innovate the company or block its use of technology through patents, harming its competitive position and results.
      "Other companies may also have (or in the future may obtain) patents or other proprietary rights that would prevent, limit, or interfere with our ability to make, use, or sell our own AI products and services."
  [1A.4] (stated) The company's ability to scale AI and cloud infrastructure is threatened by limited availability of power, land, water, and specialized chips, which could restrict its ability to train models and serve customers.
      "Failure to secure sufficient capacity in a timely manner would limit our ability to train models and serve Cloud customers."
  [1A.5] (stated) Cyberattacks, security breaches, and data privacy failures could expose sensitive user and customer information, leading to litigation, regulatory penalties, and reputational damage.
      "Systems and control failures, security breaches, failure to comply with our privacy policies, and inadvertent disclosure of user data could result in regulatory and legal exposure, seriously harm our reputation, brand, and business, and impair our ability to attract and retain users or customers."
  [1A.6] (stated) Ongoing antitrust litigation and regulatory actions regarding search, advertising technology, and app store practices could force costly changes to the business model and structural remedies that hurt operations.
      "The DOJ's remedy proposal includes structural remedies that could harm our business."
  [1A.7] (stated) A wide and growing body of laws on AI, data privacy, competition, and content moderation across many countries increases compliance costs and could force changes to products or business practices.
      "The costs of compliance with these measures are high and are likely to increase in the future, including as a result of differing, and sometimes conflicting, laws and regulations."
  [1A.8] (stated) Failure to protect patents, trademarks, and trade secrets could weaken the company's competitive advantage and value of its products and brands.
      "Any significant impairment of our intellectual property rights could harm our business and ability to compete."
  [1A.9] (inferred) Because a large share of revenue comes from outside the United States, currency fluctuations, trade barriers, sanctions, and geopolitical conflicts could reduce revenue and increase costs.
      "International revenues accounted for approximately 52% of consolidated revenues in 2025."
  [1A.10] (stated) Supply chain disruptions affecting limited-source components, especially specialized AI chips and manufacturing capacity, could delay infrastructure upgrades and product availability.
      "A significant supply interruption that affects us or our vendors could delay critical data center or network infrastructure upgrades or expansions and delay consumer product availability."
  [1A.11] (stated) Concentrated voting control by two founders through high-vote stock limits other shareholders' influence over corporate decisions, which could affect stock price if actions are viewed unfavorably.
      "This concentrated control limits or severely restricts other stockholders' ability to influence corporate matters and we may take actions that some of our stockholders do not view as beneficial, which could reduce the market price of our Class A stock and our Class C stock."
  [1A.12] (stated) Problematic or low-quality content, including spam and AI-related misuse, could damage the company's reputation and reduce use of its platforms.
      "If we fail to either detect and prevent an increase in problematic content or effectively promote high-quality content, it could hurt our reputation for delivering relevant information or reduce use of our platforms, harming our financial condition and operating results."

**Item 7** of the 10-K for FY2025, accession 0001652044-26-000018, filed 2026-02-05, source EDGAR filing archive:
  [7.1] (stated) Overall revenue grew year over year, mainly due to growth in both Google Services and Google Cloud revenues.
      "Revenues were $402.8 billion, an increase of 15% year over year, primarily driven by an increase in Google Services revenues of $37.8 billion, or 12%, and an increase in Google Cloud revenues of $15.5 billion, or 36%."
  [7.2] (stated) Cost of revenues increased, driven mainly by higher traffic acquisition costs, content acquisition costs, and depreciation expense.
      "Cost of revenues was $162.5 billion, an increase of 11% year over year, primarily driven by increases in TAC, content acquisition costs, and depreciation expense."
  [7.3] (stated) Operating expenses rose, primarily because of higher employee compensation, legal-related expenses, and depreciation.
      "Operating expenses were $111.3 billion, an increase of 22% year over year, primarily driven by increases in employee compensation expenses, expenses related to legal and other matters, and depreciation expense."
  [7.4] (inferred) Overall operating margin stayed flat even as operating income increased, since expenses grew alongside revenue.
      "Operating margin 32 % 32 % 0 %"
  [7.5] (stated) Other income and expense surged mainly due to large unrealized gains on non-marketable equity securities.
      "OI&E, net increased $22.4 billion from 2024 to 2025, primarily due to increases in net unrealized gains on equity securities resulting from fair value adjustments on non-marketable equity securities."
  [7.6] (stated) The company sharply increased capital spending on technical infrastructure and plans to increase it further next year to support AI growth.
      "we spent $52.5 billion and $91.4 billion on capital expenditures, respectively. In 2026, we expect to significantly increase, relative to 2025, our investment in our technical infrastructure, including servers and network equipment, and data centers."
  [7.7] (stated) Google Services segment profitability improved as revenue growth outpaced increases in legal, traffic acquisition, and content costs.
      "Google Services operating income increased $18.1 billion from 2024 to 2025. The increase in operating income was primarily driven by an increase in revenues, partially offset by an increase in expenses related to legal and other matters, TAC, and content acquisition costs."
  [7.8] (stated) Google Cloud segment profitability improved as revenue growth outweighed higher infrastructure usage and compensation costs.
      "Google Cloud operating income increased $7.8 billion from 2024 to 2025. The increase in operating income was primarily driven by an increase in revenues, partially offset by increases in usage costs for technical infrastructure and employee compensation expenses."
  [7.9] (stated) Other Bets losses widened mainly due to a large stock-based compensation charge tied to Waymo.
      "Other Bets operating loss increased $3.1 billion from 2024 to 2025. The increase in operating loss was primarily driven by an increase in employee compensation expenses largely due to an increase in a valuation-based compensation charge related to Waymo."
  [7.10] (stated) Operating cash flow grew substantially, reflecting higher customer cash receipts that outpaced increased cash payments for costs and expenses.
      "Net cash provided by operating activities increased from 2024 to 2025 due to an increase in cash received from customers, partially offset by an increase in cash payments for cost of revenues and operating expenses."
  [7.11] (stated) The company maintains a large liquidity position and expects existing cash and cash flow to be sufficient to meet obligations for at least the next year and beyond.
      "We expect existing cash, cash equivalents, short-term marketable securities, and cash flows from operations and financing activities to continue to be sufficient to fund our operating activities and cash commitments for investing and financing activities for at least the next 12 months, and thereafter for the foreseeable future."
  [7.12] (stated) The company raised significant new debt financing during the year to support its capital needs.
      "In 2025, we raised capital through the issuance of debt and we expect to continue to assess the use of debt and other forms of financing in the future."

ANSWER
**THESIS: GOOGL** (W-1), as of 2026-09-22

  Search and YouTube are the advertising businesses that keep growing through every cycle, the cloud segment is now profitable and taking share, and the company funds its own capital spending from operating cash flow with no debt to speak of. The thesis is that the advertising businesses keep their margins while the cloud segment's margin catches up, and that the capital spending on data centres shows in cloud revenue rather than in lasting margin damage.

What has to be true for W-1's thesis to have been right, read against Alphabet Inc.'s latest annual report. Each claim is the model's reading (claude-sonnet-5) of one section, with the passage it rests on quoted from the filing; `stated` means the passage says it in so many words, `inferred` that it is read across the section.

**Item 1** of the 10-K for FY2025, accession 0001652044-26-000018, filed 2026-02-05, source EDGAR filing archive:
  [1.1] (stated) The company operates two main Google segments plus a separate group of non-Google businesses called Other Bets, with centralized AI research supporting all of them.
      "We report Google in two segments, Google Services and Google Cloud, and all non-Google businesses collectively as Other Bets."
  [1.2] (stated) Google Services earns most of its revenue from selling performance and brand advertising placed on Search, YouTube, and partner network sites.
      "Google Services generates revenues primarily by delivering both performance and brand advertising that appears on Google Search & other properties, YouTube, and Google Network partners' properties ("Google Network properties")."
  [1.3] (stated) Beyond advertising, Google Services also makes money from consumer subscription services, app store sales, and hardware device sales.
      "consumer subscriptions, which primarily include revenues from YouTube services, such as YouTube TV, YouTube Music and Premium, and NFL Sunday Ticket, as well as Google One, which offers access to our most capable Gemini models;"
  [1.4] (stated) Google Cloud generates revenue mainly by charging customers usage-based fees and subscriptions for infrastructure, platform, and application cloud services.
      "Google Cloud generates revenues primarily from consumption-based fees and subscriptions for infrastructure, platform, applications, and other cloud services."
  [1.5] (stated) Other Bets businesses, such as autonomous ride-hailing and internet services, earn revenue mainly by selling those transportation and internet offerings to customers.
      "Revenues from Other Bets are generated primarily from the sale of autonomous transportation and internet services."
  [1.6] (inferred) Customers keep using the company's products because of continual innovation and integration of advanced AI capabilities like Gemini models into widely used products.
      "Today, all 15 of our half-billion-user products — including seven with two billion users — use our Gemini models."
  [1.7] (inferred) Advertisers keep buying because the company's advertising tools use AI to help them find effective, measurable ways to reach customers.
      "Products like Demand Gen, Performance Max, and Product Studio use the full power of our AI to help advertisers find untapped and incremental conversion opportunities."
  [1.8] (stated) Cloud customers are attracted by a broad set of AI-optimized infrastructure, developer tools, security, data, and agent products that help them innovate and cut costs.
      "Google Cloud helps customers build for the future, improve productivity, reduce costs, and unlock new growth engines."
  [1.9] (stated) Users could stop using the company's products because competing products and services are easily accessible, making user loyalty fragile.
      "users, for whom other products and services are literally one click away, on the basis of the relevance of our advertising, as well as the general usefulness, security, and availability of our products and services;"
  [1.10] (stated) The business faces intense competition across nearly every product area, including search, advertising, cloud, AI, hardware, and social media, which could draw customers away.
      "We face formidable competition in every aspect of our business, including but not limited to, from:"
  [1.11] (stated) Growing and inconsistent government regulation around AI, privacy, competition, and content could raise costs, restrict offerings, or otherwise cause customers or the business to be harmed.
      "Our compliance with these laws and regulations may be onerous and could, individually or in the aggregate, increase our cost of doing business, make our products and services less useful, limit our ability to pursue certain business practices or offer certain products and services"
  [1.12] (stated) Advertisers could shift spending away if the company fails to keep delivering ads efficiently across changing channels and user preferences.
      "advertisers, primarily based on our ability to generate sales leads, and ultimately customers, and to deliver their advertisements in an efficient and effective manner across a variety of distribution channels even as trends in advertising mediums and user preferences change;"

**Item 1A** of the 10-K for FY2025, accession 0001652044-26-000018, filed 2026-02-05, source EDGAR filing archive:
  [1A.1] (stated) The company depends heavily on advertising revenue, so losing advertisers or partners to competitors, or failing to adapt to new ad formats shaped by AI, could damage its business and financial results.
      "Failing to provide superior value or deliver advertisements effectively and competitively could harm our business, reputation, financial condition, and operating results."
  [1A.2] (stated) Large-scale spending on AI infrastructure, data centers, and long-term leasing and commercial agreements creates risk that if partners fail to perform or an industry downturn occurs, the company could be left with unusable excess capacity and unpaid obligations.
      "In such nonperformance or an industry downturn, we may incur additional liabilities, have excess capacity that we cannot easily redeploy, and not receive payments from our counterparties or customers."
  [1A.3] (stated) Intense competition, especially in AI development, could allow rivals to out-innovate the company or block its use of technology through patents, harming its competitive position and results.
      "Other companies may also have (or in the future may obtain) patents or other proprietary rights that would prevent, limit, or interfere with our ability to make, use, or sell our own AI products and services."
  [1A.4] (stated) The company's ability to scale AI and cloud infrastructure is threatened by limited availability of power, land, water, and specialized chips, which could restrict its ability to train models and serve customers.
      "Failure to secure sufficient capacity in a timely manner would limit our ability to train models and serve Cloud customers."
  [1A.5] (stated) Cyberattacks, security breaches, and data privacy failures could expose sensitive user and customer information, leading to litigation, regulatory penalties, and reputational damage.
      "Systems and control failures, security breaches, failure to comply with our privacy policies, and inadvertent disclosure of user data could result in regulatory and legal exposure, seriously harm our reputation, brand, and business, and impair our ability to attract and retain users or customers."
  [1A.6] (stated) Ongoing antitrust litigation and regulatory actions regarding search, advertising technology, and app store practices could force costly changes to the business model and structural remedies that hurt operations.
      "The DOJ's remedy proposal includes structural remedies that could harm our business."
  [1A.7] (stated) A wide and growing body of laws on AI, data privacy, competition, and content moderation across many countries increases compliance costs and could force changes to products or business practices.
      "The costs of compliance with these measures are high and are likely to increase in the future, including as a result of differing, and sometimes conflicting, laws and regulations."
  [1A.8] (stated) Failure to protect patents, trademarks, and trade secrets could weaken the company's competitive advantage and value of its products and brands.
      "Any significant impairment of our intellectual property rights could harm our business and ability to compete."
  [1A.9] (inferred) Because a large share of revenue comes from outside the United States, currency fluctuations, trade barriers, sanctions, and geopolitical conflicts could reduce revenue and increase costs.
      "International revenues accounted for approximately 52% of consolidated revenues in 2025."
  [1A.10] (stated) Supply chain disruptions affecting limited-source components, especially specialized AI chips and manufacturing capacity, could delay infrastructure upgrades and product availability.
      "A significant supply interruption that affects us or our vendors could delay critical data center or network infrastructure upgrades or expansions and delay consumer product availability."
  [1A.11] (stated) Concentrated voting control by two founders through high-vote stock limits other shareholders' influence over corporate decisions, which could affect stock price if actions are viewed unfavorably.
      "This concentrated control limits or severely restricts other stockholders' ability to influence corporate matters and we may take actions that some of our stockholders do not view as beneficial, which could reduce the market price of our Class A stock and our Class C stock."
  [1A.12] (stated) Problematic or low-quality content, including spam and AI-related misuse, could damage the company's reputation and reduce use of its platforms.
      "If we fail to either detect and prevent an increase in problematic content or effectively promote high-quality content, it could hurt our reputation for delivering relevant information or reduce use of our platforms, harming our financial condition and operating results."

**Item 7** of the 10-K for FY2025, accession 0001652044-26-000018, filed 2026-02-05, source EDGAR filing archive:
  [7.1] (stated) Overall revenue grew year over year, mainly due to growth in both Google Services and Google Cloud revenues.
      "Revenues were $402.8 billion, an increase of 15% year over year, primarily driven by an increase in Google Services revenues of $37.8 billion, or 12%, and an increase in Google Cloud revenues of $15.5 billion, or 36%."
  [7.2] (stated) Cost of revenues increased, driven mainly by higher traffic acquisition costs, content acquisition costs, and depreciation expense.
      "Cost of revenues was $162.5 billion, an increase of 11% year over year, primarily driven by increases in TAC, content acquisition costs, and depreciation expense."
  [7.3] (stated) Operating expenses rose, primarily because of higher employee compensation, legal-related expenses, and depreciation.
      "Operating expenses were $111.3 billion, an increase of 22% year over year, primarily driven by increases in employee compensation expenses, expenses related to legal and other matters, and depreciation expense."
  [7.4] (inferred) Overall operating margin stayed flat even as operating income increased, since expenses grew alongside revenue.
      "Operating margin 32 % 32 % 0 %"
  [7.5] (stated) Other income and expense surged mainly due to large unrealized gains on non-marketable equity securities.
      "OI&E, net increased $22.4 billion from 2024 to 2025, primarily due to increases in net unrealized gains on equity securities resulting from fair value adjustments on non-marketable equity securities."
  [7.6] (stated) The company sharply increased capital spending on technical infrastructure and plans to increase it further next year to support AI growth.
      "we spent $52.5 billion and $91.4 billion on capital expenditures, respectively. In 2026, we expect to significantly increase, relative to 2025, our investment in our technical infrastructure, including servers and network equipment, and data centers."
  [7.7] (stated) Google Services segment profitability improved as revenue growth outpaced increases in legal, traffic acquisition, and content costs.
      "Google Services operating income increased $18.1 billion from 2024 to 2025. The increase in operating income was primarily driven by an increase in revenues, partially offset by an increase in expenses related to legal and other matters, TAC, and content acquisition costs."
  [7.8] (stated) Google Cloud segment profitability improved as revenue growth outweighed higher infrastructure usage and compensation costs.
      "Google Cloud operating income increased $7.8 billion from 2024 to 2025. The increase in operating income was primarily driven by an increase in revenues, partially offset by increases in usage costs for technical infrastructure and employee compensation expenses."
  [7.9] (stated) Other Bets losses widened mainly due to a large stock-based compensation charge tied to Waymo.
      "Other Bets operating loss increased $3.1 billion from 2024 to 2025. The increase in operating loss was primarily driven by an increase in employee compensation expenses largely due to an increase in a valuation-based compensation charge related to Waymo."
  [7.10] (stated) Operating cash flow grew substantially, reflecting higher customer cash receipts that outpaced increased cash payments for costs and expenses.
      "Net cash provided by operating activities increased from 2024 to 2025 due to an increase in cash received from customers, partially offset by an increase in cash payments for cost of revenues and operating expenses."
  [7.11] (stated) The company maintains a large liquidity position and expects existing cash and cash flow to be sufficient to meet obligations for at least the next year and beyond.
      "We expect existing cash, cash equivalents, short-term marketable securities, and cash flows from operations and financing activities to continue to be sufficient to fund our operating activities and cash commitments for investing and financing activities for at least the next 12 months, and thereafter for the foreseeable future."
  [7.12] (stated) The company raised significant new debt financing during the year to support its capital needs.
      "In 2025, we raised capital through the issuance of debt and we expect to continue to assess the use of debt and other forms of financing in the future."

**PHI-3.2** — A company whose SIC code, as EDGAR states it, is one of the codes this clause lists is not screened by this document: the balance sheet of a bank or an insurer is its business, and the figures the other clauses read do not describe it. The codes are 6021 and 6022, commercial banks; 6035 and 6036, savings institutions; 6211, security brokers and dealers; 6311, life insurers; and 6331, fire, marine and casualty insurers. The check reports the company as excluded under this clause and reports nothing else about it. I do not own them until this document has clauses for them.
